# Ledger shortlist for Figure Ground

The 152 features the scouts said would suit this lineage (95 of them gems), in full. The whole menu is ledger-index.md.

### L001 Deep-set tube in a lifted case (colour or material, GEM)
From **01-softground Soft Ground**, round 1 (4.0, #12 of 12).
- What: The case round each monitor is lifted a step (`.crt` gradient #28322d to #202823 to #1b221d, surface #1d2421) while the glass stays today's pitch black (glass mix on #060a08). Each screen reads as a dark tube sunk into a lighter surround, like a real IBM 5151, rather than a black box on a black page.
- Files: Palette: /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-FamilyDB/62374e35-c7e3-5c25-a94d-0502bde8e9d6/scratchpad/harness/round1/palettes/01-softground.json (surface steps; screen and glass untouched). Sheet: /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-FamilyDB/62374e35-c7e3-5c25-a94d-0502bde8e9d6/scratchpad/harness/round1/out/01-softground/style.css line 1142 `.crt` background gradient, line 1139 `--phosphor-glass`.
- Shot: /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-FamilyDB/62374e35-c7e3-5c25-a94d-0502bde8e9d6/scratchpad/harness/round1/out/01-softground/shots/login.png (the sign-in face in its lifted case; lost.png too)
- Praise: Soul 4.5: "the deep monitors set in the lighter surround are a nice touch." Its designer: "the screens look even more like dark tubes set into the page." Otherwise last place.
- Caveats: It got there by lifting the whole ground, which greyed the black into 'stock slate dark mode' in every lens. Lift only the case or the bay that holds a tube, and keep the page at #0b0e0d so the green still jumps off a switched-off screen.
- Suits: Switchboard: the rack's cells in a lighter slate, each holding black glass. Flight Console: grey console bays with the tubes sunk into them. Track: the slate diagram board round the Next up tube. Figure Ground: the figure's screen sunk into its plate.

### L003 The brand is never a kind (colour or material, GEM)
From **08-colourmonitor Colour Monitor**, round 1 (5.5, #7 of 12).
- What: Outings stop sharing the signature green. Outing gets its own colour, turquoise #43dbd0 (the light cyan of an EGA screen), so any green on an idea card or in 'Lately added' means the brand and nothing else. The rest of the kind set takes a colour monitor's phosphors, deeper and more saturated rather than pastel: ideas violet #b188fe, plans sky #6ac4fb, to do yellow #f6d94f, people #fdae4f, shows #fd82ba, seasons #fc8d5e. Every CVD distance improves (kinds 13.6 to 15.8 typical, 5.2 to 9.2 protan). Pine and Alpenglow's glacier-lake teal outing (#65d1da, round 2) is the same move.
- Files: Palette: /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-FamilyDB/62374e35-c7e3-5c25-a94d-0502bde8e9d6/scratchpad/harness/round1/palettes/08-colourmonitor.json (outing, ideas, plans, todo, people, shows, seasons). harness/theme.py rewrites `.kind-outing { --kind: var(--outing); }` and `:has(.kind-outing) { --accent: var(--outing); }`. Round 2 variant: rounds/r2/palettes/r2-rand-1.json (outing #65d1da).
- Shot: /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-FamilyDB/62374e35-c7e3-5c25-a94d-0502bde8e9d6/scratchpad/harness/round1/out/08-colourmonitor/shots/home.png (Lately added: the turquoise Outing label)
- Praise: Product 6.0: "The best idea as a system for a family app ... outings get their own turquoise so the brand stops doubling as a kind ... Keep the turquoise outing and the richer sections, which are a real gain for the system." Product notes' synthesis: "08's outing with its own colour, so the brand is never a kind." Style, round 2, on Pine: "keep the glacier-teal outing, which is lovely."
- Caveats: In small labels only, it is invisible at a glance (feedback 5). Turquoise can read as a second blue beside trips' sky, or as more green to some eyes. The tritan pair show~seasonal fell to 5.4. The round 2 lesson says moving learned section colours costs points, so change outing and leave the sections the family knows. Lantern Night's persimmon outing showed that warm hues work for a kind only as small solids.
- Suits: All six have kind marks. Track: a stop's lamp ring in its kind colour, never green. Figure Ground: kind symbols in the figures' legends. Flight Console: the crew board's kind tags. Switchboard: the Ideas cell's breakdown by kind. Timetable: the enamel line colours stay apart from the phosphor. Pocket: the cartridges' labels.

### L008 Solid tiles with no outlines, outlines returned for high contrast (colour or material, GEM)
From **r6-rand-2 Night Herbarium**, round 6 (5.36, #7 of 12).
- What: What the family keeps is a matte solid tile with no border at all. Only a faint top-edge light and a bottom shadow separate it from the ground, so cards, panels and tabs read as objects rather than boxes, and the page loses a layer of line noise. On hover a tile lifts and gains an accent edge. Under `prefers-contrast: more` every tile gets its outline back.
- Files: Palette /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-FamilyDB/62374e35-c7e3-5c25-a94d-0502bde8e9d6/scratchpad/harness/rounds/r6/palettes/r6-rand-2.json (css): `--tile: inset 0 1px 0 rgb(255 255 255/.05), inset 0 -1px 0 rgb(0 0 0/.3)`; `--shadow: var(--tile), 0 1px 0 rgb(0 0 0/.55), 0 18px 30px -24px rgb(0 0 0/.95)`; `.panel, .card, .idea-card, .section-card, .tabs{border-color:transparent}`; `@media (prefers-contrast: more){.panel, .card, .idea-card, .section-card, .tabs, .chip{border-color:var(--line-2)}}`.
- Shot: /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-FamilyDB/62374e35-c7e3-5c25-a94d-0502bde8e9d6/scratchpad/harness/rounds/r6/out/r6-rand-2/shots/home.png
- Praise: Skeptic 5.0: 'the no-outline clay tiles are tasteful.' System 5.0: 'matte clay tiles with no outlines'. Designer: 'every card, panel and tab is a solid object rather than a box.'
- Caveats: The heather-plum fill (h311) read as aubergine and Dracula. With no edge, the tile must stand clear of the ground by its fill alone: slate at 7 dE or more from #0b0e0c. The amber setup panel keeps its edge (`.setup.setup`).
- Suits: Pocket Timeline: chunky moulded tiles with no outlines, like a handheld's plastic. Figure Ground: plates as solid sheets, with the figure's lines as the only lines on the page. Videotex Switchboard: cells as solid tiles when not joined in a lattice.

### L011 The family's voice in a cool solid bubble (colour or material, GEM)
From **r7-rand-6 Orchard Greenbar**, round 7 (5.53, #8 of 12).
- What: In Chat the family's bubbles are a solid, cool mist-blue fill (#1b2835) with an edge mixed 32% from the accent. Vera's bubbles stay green-black glass (#101611) with a 30% screen edge, on a thread tray. The two speakers are two materials: the family's in a grounded solid, the machine's in glass.
- Files: /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-FamilyDB/62374e35-c7e3-5c25-a94d-0502bde8e9d6/scratchpad/harness/rounds/r7/palettes/r7-rand-6.json: tokens `bubble-them:#1b2835`, `bubble:#101611`, `accent:#80a8d6`, `--mist:#1b2835`, `--glass:#101611`; css `.said-them .bubble { border-color: color-mix(in srgb, var(--accent) 32%, var(--line-2)) }`, with Vera's bubble edge at a 30% screen mix and the thread on a tray.
- Shot: /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-FamilyDB/62374e35-c7e3-5c25-a94d-0502bde8e9d6/scratchpad/harness/rounds/r7/out/r7-rand-6/shots/chat.png
- Praise: feedback 5.2: 'The three-column Home, mono figures and mist-blue family bubble are pleasant'. soul 5.8: 'the mist-blue family bubbles and thread tray are nice'. system 6.0: 'mist-blue family bubbles are a good companion'.
- Caveats: skeptic 4.9: 'the mist-blue links and bubbles are the stock slate-blue family'. The design sank on its sage trays, not on this. Nudge the blue toward denim or steel, off GitHub's navy, and keep it less saturated than the accent. Where both speakers sit on one side of a line, pair it with an edge mark (see Galley Rack's family-bubble bar) so the difference survives a squint.
- Suits: timetable: its family bubbles were a 'muddy copper-brown fill', and every lens asked for slate. Chat puts both speakers on the left, so the material has to tell them apart. track: Chat hung from the line on one side. figure: the family's lane of a sequence diagram in mist, Vera's in glass.

### L012 Warm Bakelite for the monitor case only (colour or material, GEM)
From **r9-mut-3 Bakelite Video**, round 9 (5.66, #9 of 13).
- What: The monitors' case is drawn in one warm material, oxblood Bakelite (#3a2826, C about .025, hue 15-22). It has a warm sheen (an #ffeee6 top highlight), an inner shadow at the chin, grille slots cut into the chin and the green power LED. The case is thinner and squarer than before (padding .6rem .6rem 1.9rem, radius 18/18/22/22), with flush 14px and 8px scopes on Status and Ideas. The judges' own fix points the same way: keep the material, but use less of it.
- Files: variants/r9-mut-3/palette.css: `.crt{padding:.6rem .6rem 1.9rem;border-radius:18px 18px 22px 22px;box-shadow:inset 0 1px 0 #ffeee626,inset 0 -3px 7px #0005,0 5px 0 -1px #221313,0 36px 56px -30px #000e,0 0 80px -38px color-mix(in srgb,var(--phosphor) 40%,transparent)}`, `.crt::before{left:1.3rem;bottom:.8rem;width:2.8rem;height:.3rem;background:repeating-linear-gradient(90deg,#0006 0 2px,transparent 2px 5px)}` (grille), `.crt::after{right:1.35rem;bottom:.74rem;width:.42rem;height:.42rem;background:var(--brand)}` (LED), `:is(.spend,.idea-scope) .crt{padding:10px;border-radius:14px}`. Token bezel #3a2826 in palettes/r9-mut-3.json "tokens".
- Shot: /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-FamilyDB/62374e35-c7e3-5c25-a94d-0502bde8e9d6/scratchpad/harness/tour/gallery/r9-mut-3/home.png (the Next up case)
- Praise: Style, 6: "The most material mood on the panel ... Keep the idea of a material; use less of it." Soul's fix: "If Bakelite is kept, keep it only in the monitor case (one material with a job)". Style's fix: "Keep the Bakelite as a frame and chrome material (bar, window frame lines, monitor case)". Skeptic's fix: "Oxblood only where there is Bakelite (the monitor case, perhaps the foot)." Soul, 5.6, against: "The brown cases make the tubes look like a 1970s TV cabinet".
- Caveats: Every lens marked down the oxblood filling windows, bubbles and the month (squinted, it read as Christmas). Feedback recalls that the owner said brown and warm tones do not match the phosphor, so the case is the most this material should cover. Lower the chroma further, or try a darker umber-graphite first. It answers 'more grounded coloration elsewhere' only while it stays small and solid, a material with a job.
- Suits: console: the tube bezels as the one warm material in a slate cockpit, like the Bakelite knobs of older avionics. figure: the frames round figures. switchboard: the cabinet round the map's Vera cell. timetable: Vera's own screen tile. It suits track least, since that lineage is steel.

### L013 Section-coloured band rule with a spill (colour or material, GEM)
From **r9-rand-1 Attract Mode**, round 9 (5.04, #10 of 13).
- What: Every section is a full-bleed band, painted by a ::before at inset 0 -100vmax, so there is no wrapper markup and no sideways scroll. Each band's top edge is a 2px rule at 70% of its section's colour, and an 8% wash of that colour fades into the band over 120px. Each page head is a lit band tinted 7% with the page's own section colour and ruled below the same way, so Plans' head reads periwinkle graphite, To do's brass and Ideas' lilac. The heading icon sits on a flat inlay cap in the section colour, applied without markup through `main h2 > svg.icon:first-child`.
- Files: r9-rand-1.json "css". "The frame": `main{max-width:none;padding:0 max(1rem,calc((100% - 70rem)/2)) 64px;overflow-x:clip}`. "Each band is ruled in its section's colour, and that colour spills faintly from the rule": `:where(.band,main>section.panel:not(.narrow),main>nav.panel,main>.monitors)::before{content:"";position:absolute;z-index:-1;inset:0 -100vmax;background:linear-gradient(180deg,color-mix(in srgb,var(--accent) var(--spill,8%),var(--tone,var(--bg))),var(--tone,var(--bg)) 120px);border-top:2px solid color-mix(in srgb,var(--accent) 70%,var(--rule))}` and `.setup,.home-todo{--spill:6%}`. The page head: `.page-head::before{inset:0 -100vmax;background:radial-gradient(38% 150% at 86% 0%,color-mix(in srgb,var(--accent) 10%,transparent),transparent 70%),linear-gradient(180deg,color-mix(in srgb,var(--accent) 7%,#172624),#111a18);border-bottom:2px solid color-mix(in srgb,var(--accent) 70%,var(--rule))}`. Caps: `.title-icon,.key-icon,.section-icon,.restaurants-icon` and `main h2 > svg.icon:first-child{box-sizing:content-box;padding:6px;background:color-mix(in srgb,var(--accent) 22%,#162120);border:1px solid color-mix(in srgb,var(--accent) 40%,#253534);border-radius:8px;box-shadow:var(--inlay)}`. Template: /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-FamilyDB/62374e35-c7e3-5c25-a94d-0502bde8e9d6/scratchpad/harness/rounds/r9/variants/r9-rand-1/templates/home.html (bands for Coming up, To do and Lately added).
- Shot: /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-FamilyDB/62374e35-c7e3-5c25-a94d-0502bde8e9d6/scratchpad/harness/tour/gallery/r9-rand-1/home.png (the brass, periwinkle, rose and lilac band tops). todo.png and ideas.png show the tinted page heads.
- Praise: Style, 6: "Full-width graphite bands, each ruled in its section's colour, stratify the whole page. It is a real structural idea, and it carries colour into Home, Status and Ideas." Style's fix: "Keep the full-width bands ruled in each section's colour, and the kind edges on the cards." Soul's fix: KEEP "the full-width bands ruled in each section's colour". Feedback, 4.6, against: "the band rules in five colours edge toward confetti".
- Caveats: On a teal-graphite ground the page squinted as one blue-green family, and every lens marked that down; build it on green-black slate. Five full-width colour rules on Home read as confetti. Feedback's fix is 'one steel rule with a short lead in its section's colour'; alternatively keep the full-colour rule for the page head only. Warm spills sampled bronze, so keep amber and brass at 6% or less. The bands made Home 2300px long, so pair them with two columns at 64rem and wider.
- Suits: switchboard: each page's cell carries that page's colour as a rule and spill, and the page it opens has a head in the same colour, so the map and its destinations share one key. figure: each figure's band is ruled in its section's colour. pocket: bands for NOW, NEXT and LATER, with only NOW's rule in phosphor. track: the rule's colour as the lead of that section's line.

### L014 Rules that take their part's colour (colour or material, GEM)
From **r9-rand-2 Porcelain Atlas**, round 9 (5.00, #11 of 13).
- What: One line of CSS tints every hairline on the page toward the colour of the part it belongs to. `* { --rule: color-mix(in srgb, var(--accent) 26%, var(--line)) }` resolves --rule on each element from that element's own --accent. Plans' rules lean teal, To do's straw, Ideas' violet, and the bar and Home cobalt, with no rule written per section.
- Files: r9-rand-2.json "css": `* { --rule: color-mix(in srgb, var(--accent) 26%, var(--line)); }`. Every border that uses var(--rule) picks it up: `.bar{border-bottom:1px solid var(--rule)}`, `.section-head{border-bottom:1px solid var(--rule)}`, `.tabs`, `.cards:not(.wishes){border-top:1px solid var(--rule)}`, `.said-bot .bubble`, `.settings-nav ol`, `.foot`.
- Shot: /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-FamilyDB/62374e35-c7e3-5c25-a94d-0502bde8e9d6/scratchpad/harness/tour/gallery/r9-rand-2/home.png (the head rules of The kids' wishes, Coming up, To do and Lately added)
- Praise: Soul's fix: KEEP "the rules tinted in each part's colour". System's fix: "Keep the rule tinted by its own section's colour". Skeptic's fix: keep "the star colours on rules and discs".
- Caveats: It works only where --accent is set per section or per kind; the harness already does this. Inside kind-tinted rows a rule may pick up the kind's colour; scope it with :where() if that is not wanted. At 26% the tint stays grounded; above about 35% it becomes the confetti feedback warned about.
- Suits: All six lineages, as one cheap system rule. figure: each figure's axis and tick marks lean to its section's colour. track: steel track lines lean to the colour of the section they serve. switchboard: each cell's frame leans to its page's colour. console: plate demarcation lines. pocket and timetable: the spine or thread stays steel, and each entry's rule takes its part's colour.

### L016 Section colours as a legend: key squares in the bar, caps on the phone tabs (colour or material, GEM)
From **r11-mut-2 Signal Legend, Drawn**, round 11 (6.68, #11 of 17).
- What: Each place in the desktop bar has a 7px matte key square in its section's colour before its word: steel for Vera, lilac for Ideas, cyan for Plans, lemon for To do, pink for Wishes. On the phone, each tab has a 3px cap in its colour, at 45% for the others and full for the current tab. The same colour is the one accent in that section's drawing on Home, so bar, head and drawing form one legend.
- Files: CSS in .../harness/rounds/r11/palettes/r11-mut-2.json 'css': .places a::before (desktop key square), and in the phone media block .places a::before {width:1.35rem; height:3px} with the current tab full; .../harness/rounds/r11/variants/r11-mut-2/templates/base.html
- Shot: /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-FamilyDB/62374e35-c7e3-5c25-a94d-0502bde8e9d6/scratchpad/harness/tour/gallery/r11-mut-2/settings.png (bar, x 200-620, y 15-45)
- Praise: Style: 'The desktop key squares are fine'. Interaction: keep the coloured cap on the phone tabs as a legend. Skeptic: 'five confetti key squares in the bar'.
- Caveats: Keep the icons as well (the phone tabs lost recognition with words and a cap alone). Keep the squares matte and small. Home and Vera's keys should not follow the page accent.
- Suits: switchboard: the map's cells coded with the same keys as the bar. track: each line's colour key in the legend. figure: section colours keyed from bar to figure.

### L017 Drawing-sheet material: graph paper under icons and date leaves (colour or material, GEM)
From **r11-mut-3 Blueprint Terminal**, round 11 (6.71, #10 of 17).
- What: A --sheet token draws a small blueprint drawing sheet: a 6px grid of 1px lines at 15% blueprint blue over #162030, with a 60% blueprint edge. It sits under every date leaf (1 OCT THU), row icon inlay and section-head tile, so the family's things are drawn on paper while the machine is glass.
- Files: .../harness/rounds/r11/variants/r11-mut-3/palette.css --sheet, --sheet-edge (lines 35-36), used at 449 (heads), 489 (inlays), 508 (date leaves 4.25rem), 839

- Praise: Style: 'the blueprint-grid tiles give a specific texture'. System: 'a blueprint tile heads every row and every head (about twenty identical squares)'. Feedback: 'more blue than necessary'.

### L018 Cased lens with an engraved legend strip and a dot-matrix raster (colour or material, GEM)
From **r11-wild-1 Pocket Timeline**, round 11 (5.11, #17 of 17).
- What: Each green screen sits in a slate plastic lens with a big bottom-right radius (10px 10px 42px 10px), like a handheld's screen. Across its top runs a trim strip of two thin coloured lines with a small engraved legend in a gap ('PHOSPHOR DISPLAY'). A power LED sits in the bezel's left margin. Inside, the tube adds a fine dot-matrix grid over the scanlines, so the glass reads as an LCD matrix.
- Files: .../harness/rounds/r11/variants/r11-wild-1/sheet.css .crt (lines 1156-1172: gradient slate, border-radius 10px 10px 42px 10px, 80px phosphor spill), .crt::before (the trim strip and legend, 1174-1190), .crt::after (power LED, 1192-), .crt-tube::after (the dot-matrix raster, line 1231)
- Shot: /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-FamilyDB/62374e35-c7e3-5c25-a94d-0502bde8e9d6/scratchpad/harness/tour/gallery/r11-wild-1/home.png (crop x 130-790, y 545-800)
- Praise: Soul 5.0: 'Keep ... the phosphor display in its slate bezel'. The design's tubes carry the kit's effects in full.
- Caveats: 'PHOSPHOR DISPLAY' at 8px is costume. Replace it with a legend that says something (the scale, as Long Persistence's chin does), and keep it aria-hidden. Retune the berry and indigo trims to steel hairlines. Test the raster at 1x so it does not muddy VT323.
- Suits: console: the instrument case, with the legend strip naming the readout ('SPEND · LIMIT $2.00'). switchboard: every cell's small screen in one asymmetric lens. figure: a figure plate cased as one screen. pocket: its own.

### L019 Section rules dyed from the room they lead to (colour or material, GEM)
From **r11-wild-2 Night Gallery**, round 11 (5.67, #16 of 17).
- What: Each section head hangs on a 2px rule dyed 45% of its room's pigment into the black: slate-blue for Coming up, ochre for To do, heather for Lately added, rose for the kids' wishes. On a room's own page, the rules under the tabs, filters and panels take that room's pigment. Home reads as a floor plan of the rooms the visitor meets next, in grounded, low-chroma colour that has a job.
- Files: .../harness/rounds/r11/variants/r11-wild-2/sheet.css .section-head {border-bottom: 2px solid var(--rail, color-mix(in srgb, var(--accent) 45%, var(--bg)))} with .coming / .home-todo / .lately setting --accent (lines 535-548); the same rail on tabs, the settings floor plan, filters and h2 panels (the 'Rails' rules near line 543)

- Praise: Not remarked by the judges; it was added at the critic's request. What I see: it gives colour a job without fills, which is the owner's 'more grounded coloration elsewhere'.
- Caveats: Brass and putty were too close at 45%. Check each section pair under deuteranopia. Use it on rules only, never on fills.
- Suits: switchboard: each cell's rule dyed from the page it opens, so Home is the map. track: steel lines take a tint where a line belongs to a section. timetable, pocket and figure: a section's figure rule.

### L021 Separated-block rule carrying the section colour (colour or material, GEM)
From **r12-wild-2 Ceefax Evening**, round 12 (5.88, #15 of 17).
- What: Teletext's only kind of line: a rule of separated blocks, made with repeating-linear-gradient, each block half a character cell minus a 2-3px gap. It runs under the bar in the neutral line colour, and under each section head and page head in that section's colour at 55-70%. The section's colour lives in this rule and in the drawing, not in fills.

### L025 Two inks for every printed figure, by relative colour (colour or material, GEM)
From **r13-wmut-1 Folio Switchboard**, round 13 (6.88, #9 of 17).
- What: Every printed figure (counts, drive times, due leaves, initials, day numbers) is coloured by its owner (a kind, a section or a role), but at one fixed value: --fig-ink is oklch(from owner 0.68 c*0.92 h). Folios use --folio-ink at L 0.55 and chroma times 0.72. Relative colour keeps each hue exact and its chroma, where a colour-mix toward the green-tinted graphite rotated heather toward blue and rose toward salmon. A row of figures in different hues reads as one set of inks. With prefers-contrast: more both inks switch to full colour.

- Praise: Type 5.8: 'Fading 1px traces under each Home section head give vertical structure'. Style 5.2: 'the section traces are too faint to register'. Interaction 5.4: 'too faint to help scanning'. The design sank on its lifted green-grey ground, not on the trace.
- Caveats: At 65% and 1px it vanishes at page scale; it needs about 1.5px and a brighter start, or a job that makes its fade mean something. Four section colours in a row edge toward confetti (round-5 lessons: coloured leads on Coming up and Lately added only). Never use it with Sonar Watch's lifted ground, which broke 'do not grey the black'.
- Suits: pocket: this is where it becomes information. Draw the one spine from NOW to NEXT to LATER as a phosphor trace that is brightest at NOW and decays toward LATER, so persistence is the grammar of time. track: the stretch of line behind the last passed stop fades like a trace, and the line ahead stays steel. figure: a short trace under each figure's label, in its colour, fading out before the facts.

### L060 Graduated scale with an index needle (drawing or diagram, GEM)
From **r7-rand-4 Pewter Dome**, round 7 (5.69, #7 of 12).
- What: Under each page title (and in Pewter Dome under every section head and the month head) runs an instrument scale made of pure CSS gradients: a 1px baseline, minor ticks every 6px, mid ticks every 30px and major ticks every 60px, fading out to the right. A 3px by 15px index needle in the page's own colour stands at the left end. A shorter scale, fading at both ends, sits above the footer. It is the scope's ticked rim laid flat.
- Files: /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-FamilyDB/62374e35-c7e3-5c25-a94d-0502bde8e9d6/scratchpad/harness/rounds/r7/palettes/r7-rand-4.json css, section '1. The scale': `.page-head, .section-head, .month-head { position:relative; padding-bottom:.85rem }`, `::after { position:absolute; inset:auto 0 0 0; height:7px; background: linear-gradient(var(--tick-hi),var(--tick-hi)) 0 100%/100% 1px no-repeat, repeating-linear-gradient(90deg,var(--tick-hi) 0 1px,transparent 1px 60px) 0 100%/100% 7px no-repeat, repeating-linear-gradient(90deg,var(--tick-hi) 0 1px,transparent 1px 30px) 0 100%/100% 5px no-repeat, repeating-linear-gradient(90deg,var(--tick) 0 1px,transparent 1px 6px) 0 100%/100% 3px no-repeat; mask-image: linear-gradient(90deg,#000 45%,transparent) }`, `.page-head::after { height:10px }`. Needle: `.page-head::before, .coming > .section-head::before, .lately > .section-head::before { left:0; bottom:0; width:3px; height:15px; border-radius:2px 2px 0 0; background: var(--lead, var(--accent)) }`. Footer: `.foot::before` (a 5px scale masked `transparent, #000 20%, #000 80%, transparent`). Tokens `--tick:#3a3535; --tick-hi:#574f4f`.
- Shot: /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-FamilyDB/62374e35-c7e3-5c25-a94d-0502bde8e9d6/scratchpad/harness/rounds/r7/out/r7-rand-4/shots/status.png (page head); home.png for section heads
- Praise: soul 5.3: 'the graduated scale under the heads and the graticule rings in the corner are real scope touches'. system 6.2: 'a graduated scale under every head as one consistent rule'. style 6.1: 'regular-weight Newsreader titles on graduated tick scales'. The style notes suggest it again 'under the page title only, not every head'.
- Caveats: Under every head it read as 'a perforated edge' (skeptic 4.6, interaction 5.6, system), and lessons.md lists it among the traits that faded. Use it once per page, under the masthead, or at half opacity (type fix), in steel rather than warm greige. Make the needle mean something: set its position from data (spend against the limit, the share of the week gone, the next due item), and light it #6dff9c with the kit's halo only when it shows a live reading.
- Suits: console: an instrument scale is native to a flight console. Put one under each console bay's legend, with the needle as the bay's reading (LATE, spend), so it joins the readout strip and the annunciator. figure: the axis of a figure drawn from data, for example To do's due dates as ticks on the scale with NOW as the lit needle, which is the figure the lineage promises. track: a day ruler under the fortnight line.

### L062 Time dial with signal bursts and a now needle (Next up, and the Plans month header as a tuner) (drawing or diagram, GEM)
From **r10-wild-1 Braun Signal**, round 10 (5.48, #16 of 19).
- What: A linear dial drawn in real pixels: SVG with no viewBox, each mark at its share of the width, so a line is 1px at any size and the scale words are the page's label size. It has a signal line across the glass and a 12-point burst at each day with a plan (taller for more, the next one brightest, past ones faint). There is a minor tick per day, major ticks with words (now, 1w, 2w, 3w, 4w), and a needle at now. On Home it runs along the foot of the Next up tube. On Plans (month) it becomes the month's header: 'september 2026 / 1 day with plans' at the left of a slim dial of the month's days, with bursts and the needle on today, flanked by Earlier and Later keys, so the header works as a tuner. On 404 it is a flat line with no signal.

### L065 Scope trace of the coming four weeks (drawing or diagram, GEM)
From **r10-wild-2 Bench Scope**, round 10 (5.98, #15 of 19).
- What: On Home's Next up tube the family's next 28 days are drawn as a logic-analyser trace on a scope graticule. The baseline runs from now at the left edge, where the scope 'triggers': a small triangle on the top of the glass, a dashed line down to the axis and a triangle on the axis. Each day with a plan gets a square pulse, one height step per plan that day (c1/c2/c3). The next pulse is intensified the way a scope brightens the part of the sweep it is looking at, and its day number glows. A month turn is marked on the axis ('OCT'), a plan past four weeks shows an arrow at the right edge, and the timebase '1 wk/div' is printed. The day arithmetic is done in the template (days_from_civil), so the view needs nothing new.

The 'hot' block is the promising part: let it carry data (the calendar lights a block for each day with a plan, the clipboard a row per late task), so the pictogram becomes a small figure rather than a costume. Switchboard already has pixel pictograms; these are finer, with three tones and a lit block.
- Suits: switchboard: videotex's own drawing language, sharper than its current pictograms, with each cell's picture lighting a block per count. figure: a mosaic figure whose lit blocks are the section's data is a Figure Ground figure at icon size. pocket: small mosaic marks for the NOW, NEXT and LATER groups.

### L089 Mint core, deep emerald bloom (light effect, GEM)
From **03-emeraldaura Emerald Aura**, round 1 (4.5, #11 of 12).
- What: Every bright core keeps today's #6dff9c, but its bloom is a deep saturated emerald (#12a472, hue 162, L 0.64) instead of a whitish mint fog. That is how a real tube blooms: darker and richer than the phosphor at its centre. The multiplier goes up (glow 1.15) because emerald carries a third of the mint's luminance, so the net light is 24-29% lower but reads as depth. It covers the key word, Send's ring and pool, the mark's tile, live lights, the box's focus and the greeting's pool. The tube glass takes an emerald breath too (screen-glass #04120c).

Sheet: /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-FamilyDB/62374e35-c7e3-5c25-a94d-0502bde8e9d6/scratchpad/harness/round1/out/03-emeraldaura/style.css line 79 `--glow: 0 0 0 1px rgb(18 164 114 / .402), 0 8px 28px -10px rgb(18 164 114 / .805)`; lines 198-200 `.glow { text-shadow: 0 0 .45em rgb(18 164 114 / .517), 0 0 1.3em rgb(18 164 114 / .253) }`; 263-277 `.brand-mark`; 548 button hover; 901 `.welcome` pool; 1456 `.ask:focus-within`; 1650 `.light.on`; 1139 `--phosphor-glass ... #04120c`.

- Praise: Soul 7.5: "It keeps the magic best. A mint core in a deeper, richer emerald bloom reads like a real tube." Style 5: "Bright core, deep bloom is a lovely, physically true idea." Soul notes: "Add 03's deep emerald halo, so the bloom stays rich instead of flat." Its soul fix: "Keep halo #12a472 at glow 1.15, the sea-glass wash, the teal top-glow and screen-glass #04120c."
- Caveats: It changed nothing else, so at page scale it was invisible (skeptic 2: "nearly impossible to tell from today"). At small sizes the key word's halo almost vanishes (Y 4.3 to 3.9). ### L097 Jewelled pilot lamp (light effect, GEM)
From **r9-mut-3 Bakelite Video**, round 9 (5.66, #9 of 13).

A 1.5px dark bezel ring and an 8px phosphor glow surround it. 'Off' is a hollow ring.

### L105 Light spilling out round a cut in the page (light effect, GEM)
From **r12-wild-1 Glow Ink Zine**, round 12 (4.52, #17 of 17).
- What: Wherever the machine is live (Next up, the Ideas radar, her screen, sign-in), the tube is not a pasted-on monitor. The page is cut open over it. The tube element is masked by four tiled ragged-edge SVG masks. Its wrapper carries four stacked drop-shadows: a 0.7px and a 1.2px bone-white edge, like a torn fibre catching the light, then 26px and 70px phosphor spills. So the green leaks out onto the surface round the opening, and the opening has a pale lip. Inside the opening the kit is complete: scanlines, a sheen, a vignette, a 0.35px blur on the text lines, and reverse-video labels kept crisp above the scanlines.

- Praise: feedback 3.8: 'The one thing worth carrying: the tube seen through a window cut in the page... with the tube's light spilling onto the cut edge'. soul 5.0: 'a striking scarcity device: one per page, only where the machine is live'. system 4.4 and skeptic 3.5: 'its one good idea'. lessons.md r12: 'the Zine's green tube glowing up through a torn hole was its one idea worth keeping'.

The Workbench pixel face on the glass was faulted: keep VT323.

- Caveats: Stills do not show it, so the judges took it on trust. Run it when the data changes (a new count, a new next plan), not on every page load, or it becomes noise. Reduced motion is honoured.
- Suits: console: readouts that flash when they update. switchboard: a cell's count flashing when it changes. figure: a figure's key number arriving. track: a lamp lighting as a route is set.

### L115 Outlines that light at once and fade like phosphor, with a light that orbits a cell once (motion, GEM)
From **r13-wild-4 Grid Console**, round 13 (6.16, #16 of 17).
- What: A cell is drawn by its steel outline. When pointed at, the outline jumps to its bank colour with a three-layer glow (1px ring, 18px halo, inset 22px wash) in --glow-in. When left, it decays over --afterglow (1.2s cubic-bezier(0.2, 0.7, 0.3, 1)). On a linked cell a bright point also runs once round the outline: a conic-gradient ring masked to the 1px border, turned by an @property --orbit angle from 0 to 360deg in 1s and fading at the end.

- Praise: feedback 7.2: 'the afterglow is the only one in the round you can actually see' (keep 'the visible afterglow'); lessons r13 open problems: the leaders' row afterglow was invisible for the fifth round. The orbit itself was not remarked.
- Caveats: The orbit is Tron-flavoured: keep it to linked cells, once per hover, and never under reduced motion. Without @property it degrades to a plain fade. Colour it steel for most cells and phosphor only for live ones.
- Suits: switchboard: pointing at a cell closes its circuit and a light runs round it. console: a lit key on the panel. track: a stop plate's outline lit and fading as the pointer passes. figure: plate outlines.

From **r5-rand-5 Kettle and Glaze**, round 5 (5.46, #7 of 12).

Skeptic: 'the lattice grids are an interesting mutation.'

### L136 LATER stop: hollow dashed square, line runs on dotted (state marking, GEM)
From **r11-wild-1 Pocket Timeline**, round 11 (5.11, #17 of 17).
- What: The spine down Home ends what is planned with a LATER stop: a hollow, dashed square with 'LATER  All plans →' beside it. From there the line carries on dotted to the foot of the column, because the future goes on. NOW is a steady solid lamp and NEXT a breathing green one, so the three stop states read as solid, lit, hollow.

### L137 Dark-cockpit annunciator (state marking, GEM)
From **r11-wild-3 Flight Console**, round 11 (6.67, #12 of 17).
- What: Status opens with a grid of annunciator windows, one per service. It follows the dark-cockpit convention. A window that is as it should be stays dark, with a small lit lamp and its legend in ink. A window that is off is dark, with a ring for a lamp and a quiet legend. Only a caution (amber) or an alarm (red) fills its window with light. Red means failed and nothing else, and only the chat model breathes.

Running text in plates, source notes, hints and descriptions is capped at 32em (about 75 characters), and chat lines at 36em. p and li use text-wrap: pretty with hyphens: auto, headings use text-wrap: balance, and the time-zone note is pulled up into the lede's size.

Type's notes: "Porcelain Atlas proves the owner's taste for typesetting can be served by measure and leading without a serif body; graft its measure discipline for prose (ledes, help text, Vera's replies at 60-62ch), not its serif controls."
- Caveats: The design held the whole page to one 45rem column, which ran Home to 2942px and left half the desktop empty. Apply the measure to prose elements inside a wider grid instead. Pocket and Timetable: a late row or a delayed departure in amber phosphor.

### L177 Warm solids, cool light (colour or material)
From **r2-idea-1 Lamplit Room**, round 2 (6.56, #4 of 12).
- What: A rule for where each temperature may go. Warmth is only ever opaque: ivory ink (#ddd5c4, #bfb9ab, #9c988e, #8b887e), moss-warm cards and bubbles (#131710, #191d16, #1e221b, field #0e120d), warm-stone hairlines (#2b2b24, #35352d, edge #6e6c62) and a warm monitor case. Cool colour is only ever light: an azure wash (#3d9bff at washGlow 2.6), a blue afterglow under the bar (#5a9cff at topGlow 1.5) and sky links (#7ad3ea). Nothing warm is ever diluted, so nothing samples bronze, and the room reaches below the fold on Home, Chat and Status.

Soul notes: "The best idea this round is Lamplit Room's split: warmth only in the opaque solids ... and the cool companion only as light." Round 2 lessons: "Lamplit Room (soul 8.0) kept warmth in opaque solids, cool colour in light."
- Caveats: Its solids were too quiet: cards (19,23,16) against today's (18,22,21), so style (6.3) called it 'Aurora moved into an ivory room'. The moss chroma read khaki on Status and military on the bezel; neutralise the cards (#131614, #181b19) and let the ink carry the warmth. The sky link was a stock cyan. The rule is close to the owner's later word ('bright green where it needs to be, more grounded coloration elsewhere'), so it works as a system statement, not as a look on its own.

Figure: paper-like plates as solids, the figure's live strokes lit. Pocket: the handheld's plastic warm and opaque, the screen the light.

### L179 The family's own lines in midnight blue (colour or material)
From **r2-rand-2 Late Set**, round 2 (6.86, #2 of 12).

### L180 Headband: a fading 2px cap in the card's own section or kind colour (colour or material)
From **r3-rand-1 Reading Room**, round 3 (6.04, #4 of 12 in round 3 (3.94, #12 of 12 in round 4, on its walnut and bronze)).
- What: A 2px rule rides the top edge of every panel, inset 1.25rem from each corner and fading out at both ends like a gilt rule on a book's spine. It is drawn at 75% of the panel's own --accent. So the headband is each volume's section or kind colour: pink on The kids' wishes, lemon on To do, amber on setup and restaurants, lilac on Ideas, dusk-blue on Status and Home, and on each idea card its kind (my crop shows yellow over Activity and orange over Restaurant). It puts a section's colour on the card as a thin lit edge rather than a fill.

It relies on the base sheet setting --accent per section and per idea kind. Compiled: rounds/r3/out/r3-rand-1/style.css. Re-rendered with To do, Plans and Settings in rounds/r4/out/r3-rand-1/shots/.
- Shot: /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-FamilyDB/62374e35-c7e3-5c25-a94d-0502bde8e9d6/scratchpad/harness/rounds/r3/out/r3-rand-1/shots/home.png (Lately added cards and The kids' wishes, y about 890-1260). - Praise: Style 7.4: 'the thin headband over each card is a crafted touch'. Product 5.8: 'The headbands are a nice touch'. Feedback fix: 'Keep the bronze card-edge, walnut bezel and headbands'. Style fix: 'Make the headband visible: 3px at 85%, or tint it with each section's colour so it doubles as a section marker'. It already does that on idea cards, which the judges did not notice. Soul fix: draw it in gilt. Skeptic 3.5: 'look like rendering artefacts'.
- Caveats: At 2px over a 1px border it can read as a rendering fault (skeptic). It vanished into the amber setup border (product), so leave it off .setup, .trouble and .restaurants-link. Round 5 faded 'a coloured rule along the top edge of each panel' (Heather's eight-colour selvedge threads) as confetti. Put it only on cards that carry a kind or section, at 3px, with the fade matched to the card radius, and never on every panel. Main-line lessons point the same way: 'put the kind colour in the ring lamp only', and a section colour as 'only a 2px cap and a number' was called 'truly grounded' (round 12). Drop the walnut room around it, since brown failed every round.
- Suits: switchboard: the 2px cap that won praise on each page cell, drawn as this fading headband in the page's colour and switched to phosphor with a halo on the one cell that is live. figure: a headband over each figure's band in the figure's colour, so the section reads before the number. pocket and track: plans and ideas on the spine or diagram carry their kind as a headband instead of a tinted fill.

### L190 Speaker and time set into the family slip's frame (component)

So mine and hers are told apart by material (square steel slip against round lit glass) as well as by side, and the byline takes no row inside the bubble.

### L210 Lines only where there is a sequence: setup stations and the fallback chain (drawing or diagram)
From **r9-idea-4 Signal Lines**, round 9 (6.86, #6 of 13).
- What: Lines are drawn only where the data has an order. 'Finish setting up' hangs its steps on a vertical amber line that drops from an amber ring round its icon, with a hollow amber stop per step. On the setup page the steps are numbered stations on a track: done ones are filled sage, and the next step's number is lit #6dff9c with a 10px glow. ### L215 Phosphor needle meter for today's spend (drawing or diagram)

### L229 Lit run of track: white-hot core held, then fading into the steel (light effect)
From **r13-mut-4 Track Diagram Aglow**, round 13 (7.06, #7 of 17).
- What: Where a section of line is occupied, a run of #6dff9c with a 1px white-hot core (--hot) holds for 65% of the way and then fades to transparent, so the steel rail shows through before the next stop. Live lamps breathe outward: core and tight bloom hold, and the wide halo swells from 48% to 56% and back, so a still never catches them dim.

- Caveats: As a static palette it moved least below the fold (mean dE 0.65, skeptic), so it repeated 'nearly identical'. Violet beside neon green is Dracula-adjacent (6.5 dE from #bd93f9). Make it meaning and motion, not tint: flash only on arrival (a new message, a plan just saved, a lamp that has just changed), once, and under prefers-reduced-motion show the settled green. Keep violet off the links.
- Suits: Flight Console: an annunciator lamp that has just changed flashes violet-white, then holds green, the way a master caution draws the eye. Switchboard: a cell whose count has just changed flashes once on its lamp. Track: the lamp at the stop just reached flashes and then burns steady. Night Timetable: a departure-board row that has just changed flips with a violet flash. Figure Ground: a figure's newest point flashes, then joins the drawing. Pocket: the NOW lamp as time passes a stop.

### L234 Panel switch-on cascade and anchor landing (motion)
From **r12-wmut-1 Videotex Switchboard**, round 12 (7.01, #8 of 17).
- What: As a page opens, its cells come up in reading order, 50ms apart: each starts at opacity .2 and 4px low and comes to rest in .5s, like a panel's modules warming up in turn. A cell reached by its anchor (#setup, say) flashes phosphor and fades over 1.8s ('landed').
- Files: /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-FamilyDB/62374e35-c7e3-5c25-a94d-0502bde8e9d6/scratchpad/harness/rounds/r12/variants/r12-wmut-1/sheet.css (~2075-2085): '.cell:target, .panel:target { animation: landed 1.8s }'; '.map > .cell, .main > .cells > *, .side > .cell:not(.plate) { animation: cell-on .5s ... }' with nth-child delays; '@keyframes cell-on'. @keyframes landed is defined in the same sheet's motion section.

- Praise: not remarked by the judges
- Caveats: Stills cannot show it. Cap the cascade; the sheet stops adding delay after the sixth cell. Reduced motion switches it off. The landing flash is the more useful half: any link to an anchor ('2 late' to the late rows) should land with it.
- Suits: switchboard: its own panel. console: a lamp test at power-on. figure: figures drawing in order. track: the diagram lighting section by section.

### L235 Newspaper column rules between Status's open columns (page structure)
From **r4-idea-2 Composing Room**, round 4 (6.83, #2 of 12 in round 4).
- What: On Status, the three panels (Who answers, Keys, Connected to) lose their boxes and are set as open columns under thick-and-thin head rules. A 1px vertical column rule sits in the middle of each 2.5rem gutter, from the head rule's foot to the last item, as in a newspaper. The rule appears only at 72rem and wider, so a wrapped column never draws a stray line. The page reads as a set page of three stories, not three cards.
- Files: /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-FamilyDB/62374e35-c7e3-5c25-a94d-0502bde8e9d6/scratchpad/harness/rounds/r4/palettes/r4-idea-2.json, css `@media (min-width: 72rem) { .monitors { column-gap: 2.5rem; } .monitors > .panel + .panel::before { content:""; position:absolute; top:1.1rem; bottom:.2rem; left:-1.25rem; border-left:1px solid var(--line); } }` together with the open-panel rule `.panel.panel:where(:not(.card, .idea-card, .setup, ...)) { background: var(--head-rule); border:0; border-top:2px solid var(--rule); ... }` and `:root { --rule:#4a5159; --head-rule: linear-gradient(var(--rule), var(--rule)) left 0 top 3px / 100% 1px no-repeat }`. Markup: src/familydb/web/templates/status.html `.monitors` (line 52).
- Shot: /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-FamilyDB/62374e35-c7e3-5c25-a94d-0502bde8e9d6/scratchpad/harness/rounds/r4/out/r4-idea-2/shots/status.png
- Praise: Type 8.0: 'Status has column rules'. Feedback 6.5: 'open ruled columns on Status'. Soul 7.6: Status 'as ruled columns'. Skeptic 5.9: 'ruled Status columns. It changes structure rather than tint.' Style 7.3: 'Coming up, To do and Status sit open on the black, which gives the green-on-black signature more room'. Type fix: add a hairline between columns where they wrap below 72rem. System fix: show the column rule from 52rem.
- Caveats: The box-versus-set split hangs on a long :not() list; use an explicit `.set` class (asked by four lenses). Below 72rem the columns need a top hairline instead (`@media (max-width:71.99rem) { .monitors > .panel + .panel { border-top:1px solid var(--line); padding-top:1rem } }`). Round-5 lessons: structure alone drains colour, so pair set columns with a faint slate column fill. The thick-and-thin head rule itself already spread to the main line (the Oxford rule); the column rule is the part that did not.
- Suits: timetable: a timetable is columns under rules, so column rules between time, what and who, and between days, turn the chat-as-timetable into a real printed timetable. figure: figures set side by side as stat columns separated by column rules, like a newspaper's box score. console: the instrument columns of the annunciator and crew board.

### L241 Window legend set into the frame line (page structure)
From **r8-idea-2 Reverse Video**, round 8 (7.86, #2 of 12).
- What: Every read section is a flat steel field (#171d20) with a 1px frame drawn .6rem inside it by ::before. Its heading is set into the top line after a 1.25rem stub, with the section icon in its section colour, the only place the section colour appears. The way on ('All plans ->', 'All 4', 'Add an idea / All 12', 'Change a key') is moved after its list and set into the bottom-right line. Tabs (All ideas | Restaurants, List | Month) become the window's legend, with the current tab in reverse video. Any generic .panel becomes a window, so Family, Memory, Restaurants and the idea pages inherit it with no markup of their own. It is period grammar carried by line, not by paint.

Tokens --inset .6rem, --frame #384149. Templates in rounds/r8/variants/r8-idea-2/templates/: home.html, ideas.html, plans.html, plans_month.html, status.html, tasks.html, wishes.html.
- Shot: /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-FamilyDB/62374e35-c7e3-5c25-a94d-0502bde8e9d6/scratchpad/harness/tour/gallery/r8-idea-2/home.png (Coming up, To do and Lately added windows; crop /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-FamilyDB/120fcf6f-20be-59ef-8e10-e5252598c703/scratchpad/crops/rv_coming.png); ideas.png and plans.png for the tab legends
- Praise: Skeptic (7.5): 'The only newcomer whose rules would hold on any page'. Style (7.5): 'the most CRT-native idea: the page is drawn in the grammar of Turbo Vision-era software, not in more green'. Soul (8.2): 'the most CRT personality of the field without adding a drop of green'. Round 9's Signal Legend carried it as rail legends.
- Caveats: Legends at .88rem/600/.07em mono caps shout ('THE KIDS' WISHES'). Keep 'All ...' at the head's right as an underlined sentence-case link. Keep tabs as keys with a 44px hit area and a visible edge on the unselected one. - Suits: Videotex Switchboard: each page cell becomes a window with its number and name in the top frame and its lamp and count in the bottom frame. Flight Console: bays with the legend engraved into the bay's frame line instead of a caps heading on top. Figure Ground: plates with 'FIG 3' and the plate's name set into the frame. Night Timetable: timetable boards titled in the frame.

### L248 An edge bar that marks the family's own bubble (state marking)
From **r7-idea-1 Galley Rack**, round 7 (8.23, #1 of 12).
- What: The family's chat bubble keeps a 1px line edge and gets a 3px accent (periwinkle) bar drawn as an inset shadow on its right edge, the side facing the speaker's avatar. Type, 7.4: "label-over-data (the date above the title) with tabular facts on the right. It is disciplined and new." System's fix: keep "label-over-data (mono label line above a sans title)". lessons.md lists "label-over-data rows" among the type traits the panel rewarded.
- Caveats: Interaction: "the label-over-data rows put 'ALEX' before the task"; on To do put the title first and the label under or beside it. Type: trim each caps line to two tokens ('ALEX · DUE 27 SEP') and drop the OPEN chip. Mono caps on every row got noisy. Type also suggests Plex Mono in place of Martian Mono, so the sans and mono are one superfamily.
- Suits: pocket: NEXT and LATER entries with the when over the what, the label line acting as the spine's scale. track: station boards with the time over the place. console: flight-plan legs. figure: captions over values. timetable: the said-by as a label line over each bubble.

### L265 Legend tables with true column heads and a split drive column (typography)
From **r11-idea-2 Long Persistence**, round 11 (7.88, #2 of 17).
- What: Every list is a table under a column-head row set in Archivo wdth 112, 600, 0.75rem caps, over a 1px rule: DAY PLAN TIME IN on Coming up, TASK WHO DUE on To do, KIND IDEA DRIVE WAY FOR # on Ideas. The prose drive ('about 2 h 10 min south of home (estimate)') is split into a right-aligned tabular figure ('2 h 10 min') and a compass column ('S'), with the full sentence kept for screen readers.
- Files: .../harness/rounds/r11/variants/r11-idea-2/templates/ideas.html (the minutes() macro and drive/way parsing, lines 12-21 and 137-160), home.html, tasks.html; .../r11-idea-2/palette.css (the 'Print' rules for column heads, zebra and figures)
- Shot: /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-FamilyDB/62374e35-c7e3-5c25-a94d-0502bde8e9d6/scratchpad/harness/tour/gallery/r11-idea-2/ideas.png (x 57-1210, y 655-900)
- Praise: Type 8.2 (top four, promise): 'the best new typesetting idea this round ... the typeset system worth writing into the standard'. Interaction 8.2: 'the column heads ... make every list and Status say what each fact is'. Usability 8.5 (top four): 'a tired reader scans down one column'.
- Caveats: Status's three narrow tables wrapped every other cell; use full-width tables or drop the LAMP column. The drive parsing belongs in views.py. It may already be grafted into the main line's leader.
- Suits: track: one header row per plate group, with times in the timetable margin. timetable: a header over the margin. console: crew board and log columns. switchboard: cell counts as a table. figure: the legend under a figure.

### L267 Slashed zero on the tubes (typography)
From **r11-mut-1 Track Diagram, Large Setting**, round 11 (7.11, #5 of 17).
- What: VT323's 'zero' feature turns on a slashed zero on every tube (4Ø4, 17:ØØ on Next up, 1Ø places, the spend), as terminals drew it. DM Mono's stamps were already slashed.
- Files: .../harness/rounds/r11/variants/r11-mut-1/src/track.css font-variant-numeric: tabular-nums slashed-zero on .crt-tube, .crt .radar-say and the figure classes (lines 60-64)
- Shot: /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-FamilyDB/62374e35-c7e3-5c25-a94d-0502bde8e9d6/scratchpad/harness/tour/gallery/r11-mut-1/home.png (the Next up tube, x 730-1155, y 185-410: '17:ØØ')
- Praise: Soul: 'the slashed Ø on the tubes is a nice terminal touch'. Style: 'the slashed zero is invisible off the tubes'.
- Caveats: The shipped static/fonts/vt323.woff2 lacks the feature; the full VT323 must ship. Videotex argued the family's own words should keep a plain zero; keep it to the machine's glass.
- Suits: console and figure: readouts and scopes. switchboard: cell counts on glass. Any tube.

### L268 The tube keeps only what it shows; how to read it goes under the case (typography)
From **r11-mut-4 Track Diagram Relit**, round 11 (7.52, #3 of 17).
- What: On Ideas' radar, the reading note ('North at the top. Rings at 15 min, 45 min and 2 h by road.') was the least legible text on the glass. It moved out of the tube into a caption under the case, set in DM Mono 0.75rem in the label steel at 34ch with text-wrap: pretty. The tube keeps only its data, at the 1.2rem floor, and the cursor moves to the end of its last line.
- Files: .../harness/rounds/r11/variants/r11-mut-4/src/phosphor.css .radar-caption (line 78); .../r11-mut-4/templates/ideas.html
- Shot: /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-FamilyDB/62374e35-c7e3-5c25-a94d-0502bde8e9d6/scratchpad/harness/tour/gallery/r11-mut-4/ideas.png (x 817-1193, y 240-505)
- Praise: Not remarked by the judges. What I see: a clean typographic rule for every tube (data on the glass, reading on the page).
- Caveats: Keep the caption short and tie it to the tube (a figure with a figcaption).
- Suits: figure: its rule (figures carry data, captions carry keys). console: placards under instruments. switchboard and track: under the monitor.

### L269 The largest type step belongs to the machine (typography)
From **r11-wmut-1 Tuning Dock**, round 11 (6.76, #8 of 17).
- What: A perfect-fourth scale of six named steps (12 note, 16 text, 21.3 head, 28.4 title, 37.9 page, 50.5 show) covers every size on 19 pages, audited by a script. Every h1 is the page step. The largest step, 'show', is reserved for the one figure a tube shows (the day's spend, the To do tally, the Restaurants tally, the 404), so the biggest thing on any page is lit. Other hierarchy uses Archivo's width axis: 74-78% for display, 100% for reading, 106-118% for small caps.
- Files: .../harness/rounds/r11/variants/r11-wmut-1/palette.css :root --fs-note ... --fs-show with the comments (lines 23-28), h1 / h2 (74-75), the tally tube (716-); audit scripts .../r11-wmut-1/sizes.sh, sizes.js; build.py moves today's sizes onto the scale
- Shot: /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-FamilyDB/62374e35-c7e3-5c25-a94d-0502bde8e9d6/scratchpad/harness/tour/gallery/r11-wmut-1/settings.png ($0.00 at the show step in the tube, the page title at the page step)
- Praise: System 7.0: 'on a perfect-fourth scale'. Type 6.2: 'a good idea, but its low steps are too small'; insert a 15px step.
- Caveats: Add a 15px step between 16 and 12, so facts and model names are not at the floor. The show step on a tube must stay 1.2rem+ VT323 legible.
- Suits: console: readouts are the biggest type on the page. figure: a figure's number outranks its head. switchboard: cell counts at the show step on glass. Any lineage's written scale.
